el.offsetHeight; // commit the start position\nsrc/Karaoke.tsx:225:    const t = f.time + (timeline.track.offset ?? 0);\n# LyricGlow\n\nA personal stage karaoke player. Drop in an MP3 or MP4, load (or tap out) word‑timed lyrics, turn on the mic, and sing — with a conductor cue ball that leads every syllable, lyrics that light up with the tune, a SingStar‑style pitch lane, live scoring and an end‑of‑song grade, all on a lacquered stage that pulses with the music.\n\nBuilt with React 19, TypeScript and Vite, plain CSS, **no runtime dependencies** beyond React, and nothing persisted in the browser (no localStorage / IndexedDB / cookies — files you export are downloaded to you).\n\n```bash\nnpm install\nnpm run dev          # http://localhost:5173\nnpm run build        # tsc -b && vite build\nnpm run lint         # eslint (react-hooks v7 + react-refresh)\nnpm run check:model  # fixture checks: syllables, LRC, round-trips, pitch, scoring\n```\n\n## What it does\n\n| Area | Details |\n|---|---|\n| **Playback** | Local MP3/MP4 via `URL.createObjectURL` (video becomes the full‑stage background). The built‑in demo plays a **synthesized melody + beat** (Web Audio, generated from each syllable's note) on a virtual clock, so ▶ is never silent; switch it off under **More → Singing**. Play / pause / restart / seek on the thumb bar; tempo 0.5–2× under **More → Speed**. |\n| **Karaoke animation** | Syllable‑level data; words render joined (\"Softly\", not \"Soft ly\"). Each syllable reveals left→right over its own duration (slow syllables slowly, fast ones fast). The cue ball arcs to the next syllable `0.32 s / rate` ahead of the voice and bounces once per syllable. Finished words drop and leave a translucent ghost behind; the last word of each line gets a bounce/stretch/shake peak. Lines glide between slots. |\n| **Find a song** | **Find song** opens a sheet: search synced lyrics on [LRCLIB](https://lrclib.net) (free, community, no key; several query shapes are tried and merged) and load them with one tap; plain-text results jump into Sync Studio. Add a backing track from a file or from **YouTube** — with a `VITE_YT_API_KEY` set, the app searches YouTube in place (Music category only) and lists **only YouTube Music tracks (\"Artist - Topic\" art tracks), VEVO/official videos and the artist's own channel**, ranked by closeness to the lyrics' length, so the recording is the standard one and the words line up; karaoke, covers, live and sped-up uploads are hidden behind \"Show other uploads\". An official upload that refuses embedding is skipped automatically for the next one. Without a key it opens YouTube pre-searched and you paste the link. The video becomes the stage and the karaoke syncs to its clock. Picking a video **auto-matches its lyrics** (title/artist cleaned from the video, LRCLIB version chosen by closest length); a banner warns whenever the demo lyrics are still showing over a real song; lyric results flag the version that \"matches length\" of your backing track. **Tap to sync** (More → Lyrics timing, or the \"Different version?\" hint) shows the next line and sets the lyric offset from one tap when the singer starts it; ±0.5 s chips and <kbd>[</kbd>/<kbd>]</kbd> fine-tune. |\n| **Voice level** | For your own MP3/MP4: a **Voice** slider (default 35 %) uses centre-channel reduction to turn the original singer into a faint guide while keeping the band and the bass. Auto-bypasses on mono files. Not possible for YouTube (browsers hide its audio). |\n| **Song sync** | Import `.lrc` (standard `[mm:ss.xx]` and enhanced `<mm:ss.xx>` word timing, Walaoke `M:`/`F:`/`D:` prefixes, `[v:]` tags, `[Chorus]`‑style section markers, `[offset:]`) or LyricGlow `.json`. Plain‑timed lines get syllable timing from a built‑in English syllable estimator. Export enhanced LRC or lossless JSON. |\n| **Sync Studio** | Paste lyrics, pick voices per line, play the song and tap <kbd>Space</kbd> on every syllable (<kbd>Enter</kbd> ends a line, <kbd>Backspace</kbd> undoes, <kbd>Esc</kbd> play/pause, <kbd>,</kbd>/<kbd>.</kbd> adjust tap latency). Review with ±10/±50 ms nudge, re‑tap from any line, download, or use it in the player. The stage previews your timing live. |\n| **Lyrics styles** | **KaraFun** (default): three equal lines of bold rounded sans, white with a soft shadow, each word wiped with the singer's colour as it is sung; sung words and lines keep their colour and the finished line slides up out of view; dots count in before an entry; no cue ball. **Classic**: the original LyricGlow stage with the cue ball, word fall and ghosts. Switch under More → Display → Lyrics. |\n| **Phone apps** | Installable **PWA** (manifest, icons, offline app shell via a service worker; \"Get the app\" under More shows Android's install prompt or the iPhone Add-to-Home-Screen steps) plus **Capacitor** projects in `android/` and `ios/` for Play Store / App Store builds — see `docs/mobile-apps.md`. |\n| **Listen mode** | Shazam-style: play the song anywhere (YouTube Music, Spotify, a speaker, a karaoke machine), tap **Listen & follow** in the song sheet, and LyricGlow records 6 s from the mic, recognises the song **and its position** through ACRCloud, loads the LRCLIB lyrics and starts the lyric clock there. It re-checks every 25 s and nudges the clock by the measured drift (seeks only for jumps > 1.5 s), pauses checks while paused, and tells you when it can't hear a song. The scoring mic stays separate. Needs the server keys below; without them the button says so. |\n| **Guide cue** | Over a real backing track (file or YouTube) a very quiet synthesized **guide**: a soft hum breathes in 0.22 s before every line (pitched to the line's first note when the lyrics carry one, else a gentle pitch per voice) and a faint tick marks each word onset — an audible hint for your entry alongside the cue ball. Default 25 %, slider under More → Singing, 0 = off. Mixed straight to the speakers, never into the stage analyser. |\n| **Mic & scoring** | Web Audio pitch detection (McLeod Pitch Method, hand‑written). Pitch lane under the lyric with target bars per syllable, folded to one octave so your register doesn't matter. Per‑syllable pitch + timing scoring (timing‑only when a syllable has no note), combo multiplier, line ratings, S–E grade with a \"Partial\" flag. Easy/Normal/Hard tolerances and a mic‑latency slider. Nothing from the mic is routed to the speakers. |\n| **Stage** | A toned-down abstract **mosaic** (jittered triangular lattice in the palette at ≤ 10 % alpha, drifting and breathing with the song) fills the stage when no video is showing. Audio‑reactive footlights and bass‑pulsing spotlights/vignette (from an analyser on your media, or a BPM‑synced pulse for the demo), 3‑2‑1 count‑in before lines after a gap, section title cards with a light sweep, ember bursts on peak words, a golden finale, fullscreen performance mode that hides the chrome after 3 s idle. Dark/light themes; lyrics sit in the upper half of the screen with the controls on a bottom **thumb bar** (Song · Restart · Play · oblong mic switch · More), so a phone held in one hand works in portrait and landscape. Honors `prefers-reduced-motion` plus an in‑app toggle. |\n\n### YouTube search key (optional, 5 minutes)\n\n1. Enable the API: <https://console.cloud.google.com/apis/library/youtube.googleapis.com> (create a project if asked).\n2. Create a key: <https://console.cloud.google.com/apis/credentials> → *Create credentials → API key*.\n3. Restrict it: *Websites* → your domain (e.g. `https://lyricglow.vercel.app/*`); *API restrictions* → YouTube Data API v3.\n4. Set `VITE_YT_API_KEY=<key>` in Vercel (Project → Settings → Environment Variables) or in a local `.env.local`, then rebuild. The free quota is ~100 searches/day; the manual paste flow keeps working when the quota is spent.\n\n### Typical flow for a real song\n\n1. **Find song** → either search the lyrics first (type title + artist → **Use**), or go straight to step 2 and pick a YouTube video — the lyrics are matched automatically.\n2. Backing track: **Audio / video file** from your device (then set **Voice** to taste), *or* tap a suggested YouTube video (with a key) / **Find on YouTube ↗** → paste the link → **Load**.\n3. ▶ Play. If the words run early/late, **More → Tap to sync** and tap when the singer starts the shown line. Slide the mic switch on, sing.\n\nYouTube notes: the player must stay visible (YouTube terms), the stage lights follow the song's BPM rather than the audio (browsers don't expose YouTube's sound), tempo snaps to YouTube's rates (0.5/0.75/1/1.25/1.5/2), and videos whose uploader disabled embedding show an error — try another upload.\n\n### Listen mode setup (ACRCloud)\n\nRecognition runs through `api/recognize.ts`, a Vercel function that signs the request so the secret never reaches the browser. Create an ACRCloud account → Console → **Audio & Video Recognition** → new project (bucket *ACRCloud Music*, type *Audio*) and set three **server-only** environment variables on the Vercel project (no `VITE_` prefix):\n\n```\nACR_HOST=identify-eu-west-1.acrcloud.com   # the host shown in your project\nACR_ACCESS_KEY=…\nACR_ACCESS_SECRET=…\n```\n\nThe function accepts an 8 kHz mono 16-bit WAV of ≤ 600 KB, rate-limits to 20 requests per minute per IP, and returns `{ ok, match | null, code }`. ACRCloud's `db_end_time_offset_ms` / `sample_end_time_offset_ms` give the track position at the end of the sample; the client adds the elapsed time since the capture ended. On iPhone, Safari may pause music playing on the same phone when the mic opens — put the music on another device or a speaker.\n\n## Keyboard (player)","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"d2ce4b07-2c04-4b8f-aec0-e36793f7dd12","timestamp":"2026-11-10T22:28:31.028Z","serverClassifierContext":{"request":"53f82c50-bdbf-47e7-8e3d-e24d52fd990d","context":{"git_state":{"cwd":"/home/user/DeepSeek-R1","root":"/home/user/DeepSeek-R1","branch":"claude/lyricglow-karaoke-app-ugIUj","default_branch":null,"status":{"clean":false,"counts":{"staged":0,"modified":1,"untracked":null,"untracked_normal":2},"porcelain":null,"truncated":false},"visibility":{"origin":{"host":"github.com","remote":"DenXio101/DeepSeek-R1","visibility":"unknown"},"push_remote":"origin","remotes":[{"name":"origin","host":"github.com","remote":"DenXio101/DeepSeek-R1","visibility":"unknown"}],"visibility_cache":[{"host":"github.com","remote":"DenXio101/DeepSeek-R1","visibility":"unknown"}]}},"live_cwd":"/home/user/DeepSeek-R1","platform":"linux"}},"sandboxed":false,"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b5483a5f-3f19-4686-a67d-ac48062112b7","permissionDecision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reasonType":"classifier"},"userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote_mobile","cwd":"/home/user/DeepSeek-R1","sessionId":"48275c69-5936-5c57-b5a4-9fb9a672450d","version":"2.1.322","gitBranch":"claude/lyricglow-karaoke-app-ugIUj","slug":"was-just-thinking-if-synchronous-plum"}

{"parentUuid":"69fb45d9-1c9c-49cc-81a0-3487255c9cfb","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a83f10a1b14fec1c4","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011Cgv383xaNGUvbMDYrjk4D","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_017rrN1BbmDCeTY733MtScXv","name":"SubagentHandback","input":{"message":"STAGE 8 ZOOM-OUT REVIEW (REVIEW mode)\nTarget: design_draft.md (362 lines). Consolidated report read as data. Goldfish code checked at /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow (App.tsx, AudioGraph.ts, PlaybackEngine.ts, KaraokeSession.ts, MediaElement.tsx, timeline.ts, README.md). Read-only; nothing modified.\n\nShort verdict: Not yet. The root problem is \"give a local-file singer a KaraFun-style player\". The design solves it, but only by bundling three features of very different risk into one dependency chain. With the consolidated fixes applied as written, some of them collide (findings 2, 3, 4, 5). Once the conflicts are resolved the way I recommend below, and Key is decoupled from the queue refactor, the design is the simplest workable shape. Details at the end.\n\nFINDINGS\n\n1. Blocker. Key is made dependent on the queue refactor.\nScope: lines 115, 119-121, 288 (`SongEntry.settings {key, rate, vocalLevel}`), 341 (#31, `keyOffset` read from `current.settings.key`), and phase order 241-348 (Key is Phase 5, behind Phases 2-4).\nProblem: the consolidated report never raised this. The riskiest feature (the worklet) cannot ship until the largest refactor (`SongQueue`, `SongFlow`, URL ownership, element reuse) has landed. The per-entry `settings` object is also speculative. By D10 (lines 119-121) key and vocal level carry over from song to song and rate resets, so nothing ever differs per song. Key and vocalLevel are session state, exactly like `vocalLevel` is today (`App.tsx:83`).\nFix: hold `keyOffset` in App next to `vocalLevel`; carry-over then comes for free and the queue just does not reset it. Drop `SongEntry.settings`; `advance()` only resets rate. Reorder to: Vocals, then Key (with its spike), then Queue and Intro. Key then has no dependency on Phases 2-4, and each slice can be reverted on its own.\n\n2. Blocker. Conflict: B2 (\"keep the gate out of the engine\") versus the design's `setPlayGate` (lines 108, 282). B2 wins.\nReason: the gate adds the most engine state (`playToken`, `pendingPlay`, `gated` in the snapshot) to the one component every consumer depends on. It also breaks every reader of `playingFlag` (toggle, Space, `k`, ThumbBar), and the engine would have to learn about queues, which is the wrong layer.\nRequired refinements to B2:\n(a) The pre-roll is App-level state, not an engine mode: one `introActive` boolean plus the card's timer. When the countdown ends or the user taps, the card calls the existing `engine.play()`.\n(b) Do the iOS prime inside the user's tap (muted `play()`, then pause, then seek 0, on the same element). Without it, calling `play()` from a timer is the Q2 failure on the very first song. Move the iOS check to the phase that introduces the intro, not the queue phase.\n(c) Pause, Seek and Restart simply dismiss the card.\n(d) Delete the `gated` snapshot field and the engine fixture on lines 294 and 282.\n\n3. Major. Conflict: M2 (\"apply `keyOffset` in `buildTimeline`\") versus B5's `effectiveKey`. Keep the single `effectiveKey`, but M2's mechanism is wrong.\nScope: lines 97-102, 339; `App.tsx:109`, `useKaraokeSession.ts:10`, `PitchLane.tsx:63,105-110`.\nProblems found in the code:\n(i) `timeline` is a `useMemo` and `KaraokeSession` is created `useMemo(() => new KaraokeSession(timeline), [timeline])`. The guide and demo synths are also rebuilt on `timeline` (`App.tsx:198-217`). Rebuilding the timeline on every key tap would therefore reset the running score and tear down both synths mid-song.\n(ii) `PitchLane` reads raw `lines[].syllables` and `s.note`, not `timeline.flat`. A transposed `flat` would not reach it.\nFix: transposition is applied at one read point without rebuilding. Give the timeline a settable `keyOffset` with a `noteOf(syl)` accessor (the `session.setScoring(...)` pattern already in the codebase), and make PitchLane, GuideSynth, DemoSynth and scoring call it.\n`effectiveKey` is the only value ever passed in: 0 when the source is YouTube/Listen, when the worklet failed or is bypassed, or when `setKey` has not yet resolved; otherwise the user's key. For the demo track it equals the user's key (synth only). Because the worklet loads asynchronously, the pitch lane and scoring must not move until the shifter is actually in the graph.\n\n4. Major. Conflict: M1 (one flag) versus G5 (every flag off equals today). Resolve by narrowing G5 and ungating Vocals and Queue.\nScope: lines 34, 137, 183, 189, 269.\nProblem: G5 promises a guarantee the design cannot keep. Phase 2 is explicitly ungated (line 269), and the Vocals slice moves Restart (a visible behaviour change). A flag cannot make \"exactly as today\" true for those.\nFix: G5 becomes \"with `VITE_FEATURE_KEY` off, the audio path (graph topology, `preservesPitch`, clock) is identical to today\". Vocals and Queue ship ungated and are guarded by the baseline flows, not flags. `VITE_FEATURE_KEY` defaults to 0 on web and native until Q1 and Q3 are measured; today the web default is 1 while Q1 says \"before it defaults on\". Delete the `?feature=` override. State plainly that native rollback is a store release, not a redeploy.\n\n5. Major. Conflict: B4 (one persistent `<video>` for every file) versus the existing `audio-element` testid flows. B4's fix loses; its diagnosis is half right.\nScope: lines 123, 290; `MediaElement.tsx:20-28`.\nReasoning:\n(a) The design's stated reason for one element (a memory \"leak\", line 162) is weak. `mediaSources` is a `WeakMap` and unreferenced elements are collected.\n(b) The real reason is iOS element activation (Q2): a fresh element has no prior user-initiated play, so `advance()` autoplay can be blocked.\n(c) Switching every MP3 user to a `<video>` element changes the default path, against the \"key 0 equals today\" rule, to cover a mixed-kind set.\n(d) The `audio-element` testid is defined only in `MediaElement.tsx`. The consumers live in the scratchpad flows that Step 0 copies in, so check them there.\nFix: keep the audio/video split and key the element by kind instead of URL, so same-kind songs (the common case) swap `src` on one element and keep one chain. A mixed-kind switch accepts the already-planned \"tap to play\" fallback on iOS. Rewrite Alternative D's reason to iOS activation. In either option the engine still needs an explicit reset on `src` swap, because `attachMedia` returns early on the same element (`PlaybackEngine.ts` `attachMedia`) and would not bump `generation`, `ended` or `lastMediaTime`.\n\n6. Major. B1 (latency) is right, but the fix should live in the engine frame, not the graph.\nScope: lines 31, 93, 337; the eight sites reading `f.time + offset` (`Karaoke.tsx:225`, `SpectrumCanvas.tsx:53`, `PitchLane.tsx:55`, `PitchTracker.ts:56`, `GuideSynth.ts:134`, `DemoSynth.ts:131`, `KaraokeSession.ts:222`) plus ProgressRow.\nFix: one shared `lyricTime(frame, track)` helper in `model/timeline.ts`. The engine exposes `setOutputLatency(seconds)` and writes it into `Frame` (already in song-seconds, so rate scaling is handled once). The helper then needs no graph reference. The tracker already has a setter pattern (`tracker.setOffset`, `App.tsx:123`); give it the same treatment. Drop \"unaffected by construction\" (line 18), since the design does change the compensated time. Treat the 30 ms goal as unconfirmed until the Q3 measurement, and measure at 0.5×, 1× and 2×.\n\n7. Major. The whole Key slice has no go/no-go gate and no fallback.\nScope: lines 90-91, 327-348, Q3 (line 50).\nProblem: three unproven assumptions carry G2. The first is that `preservesPitch = false` plus a rate mirror behaves correctly behind a `MediaElementSource`. The second is that the group delay is a stable constant. The third is that the worklet is not too heavy for a phone. The design only measures these at the end of Phase 5, after the queue refactor is done, and says what to do only if the worklet fails to load, not if it works poorly.\nFix: make a spike the first step of the Key slice (Chrome, Firefox, iOS Safari, Android; pitch error in cents at 0.5×, 1× and 2×; the latency click test). State the exit: if the spike fails, Key ships only on the demo track (synth, no worklet) or is dropped, and Vocals and Queue ship regardless. This is also where B5 resolves: drop the in-worklet auto-bypass (`performance.now` does not exist in the worklet scope) and keep one manual \"Key off\" path.\n\n8. Major. `SongQueue` as a state-owning class, plus `SongFlow`, is the wrong altitude for what the app needs.\nScope: lines 26, 105-123, 269-296.\nProblem: `source` and `track` are plain App states changed in several places (`App.tsx:90,70`). The design adds a class, a snapshot, a hook, a reducer and `setCurrentSong`, then converts four callers. `SongFlow` has six phases, but `finished`, `gradeReadyKey` and `gradeDismissed` already exist (`App.tsx:85,140,629`) and are only restated (B3).\nFix: a minimal `useSongSet` hook holding `{ entries: {file, track, title}[], index }`, which owns the single object URL and cap checks, and nothing else. Non-queue flows (`handleYouTube`, `startListen`, file upload) keep `setSource` and `setTrack`; add a per-call-site table. `SongFlow` collapses to the `introActive` flag from finding 2 beside the existing session flags. The Phase 2 \"net-neutral App.tsx\" cap (719 lines) cannot hold; replace it with a rule of no new state in App beyond the hook.\n\n9. Major. Scope still beyond the stated goals after the fixes.\nScope: lines 133, 135-137, 98-100 (hotkeys), 345, 261, 283.\nCut or defer: the `Diagnostics` module with its export UI, `window.__lg` and JSON download (use console counters and a dev-only dump); the `PitchShifter` interface (contradicts the Non-Goal on line 43; use a concrete `SoundTouchShifter`); the `v`, `-` and `=` hotkeys; `?feature=`; move-up and move-down buttons in the queue (remove and re-add covers 8 entries); code-level bodies in the plan (the `read()` helper, `toggleVocals`, the `play()` body) that belong in the PR, not the design.\nKeep: the latency click-test script (it measures Q3) and the three small UI changes.\nAlso fix one scope rule: the intro should appear only when a set is started, not for every single-file Play. Line 298 (\"gates the card for single songs too\") makes every Play wait about 2 s, which changes the default experience and has no flag after finding 4.\n\nOTHER CONFLICT CHECKS (no separate finding needed)\n- B5 `effectiveKey` and M2 agree once finding 3's mechanism is used.\n- B6 (flows do not exist) overlaps finding 7: write the flows from the assertions already listed (lines 262-263, 306-307, 322-323, 343-344) before relying on \"baseline unchanged\" gates.\n\nDOES THE DESIGN SOLVE THE ROOT PROBLEM IN THE SIMPLEST WAY AFTER FIXES?\nYes for Vocals (a bar and level toggle on an existing value), and yes for Key if the worklet passes the spike, since there is no simpler way to keep tempo while changing pitch (Alternative A moves tempo; verified in `PlaybackEngine` rate handling). Queue and Intro become simple once reduced to: one hook, one boolean for the card, the existing `engine.play()` at countdown end, and an element keyed by kind. The remaining cost is the new runtime dependency, which contradicts the README line \"no runtime dependencies beyond React\" (README.md line 5). That needs an explicit decision paragraph, a README edit, an `import()` for lazy loading, and the precache decision for the processor file, rather than a silent change.\n\nRelevant files:\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/egm/karafun-set/design_draft.md\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/src/App.tsx (lines 83, 85, 90, 109-123, 140, 198-217, 629)\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/src/engine/PlaybackEngine.ts\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/src/engine/KaraokeSession.ts (line 222)\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/src/engine/useKaraokeSession.ts (line 10)\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/src/engine/AudioGraph.ts\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/src/components/MediaElement.tsx\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/src/components/PitchLane.tsx (lines 63, 105-110)\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/src/model/timeline.ts"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":5755,"cache_read_input_tokens":112997,"output_tokens":7893,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":2957},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":5755},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":7893,"cache_read_input_tokens":112997,"cache_creation_input_tokens":5755,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":5755,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard","fallback_credit":null},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"toolInputStreamMs":35760,"wireToolInputs":{"toolu_017rrN1BbmDCeTY733MtScXv":{"message":"STAGE 8 ZOOM-OUT REVIEW (REVIEW mode)\nTarget: design_draft.md (362 lines). Consolidated report read as data. Goldfish code checked at /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow (App.tsx, AudioGraph.ts, PlaybackEngine.ts, KaraokeSession.ts, MediaElement.tsx, timeline.ts, README.md). Read-only; nothing modified.\n\nShort verdict: Not yet. The root problem is \"give a local-file singer a KaraFun-style player\". The design solves it, but only by bundling three features of very different risk into one dependency chain. With the consolidated fixes applied as written, some of them collide (findings 2, 3, 4, 5). Once the conflicts are resolved the way I recommend below, and Key is decoupled from the queue refactor, the design is the simplest workable shape. Details at the end.\n\nFINDINGS\n\n1. Blocker. Key is made dependent on the queue refactor.\nScope: lines 115, 119-121, 288 (`SongEntry.settings {key, rate, vocalLevel}`), 341 (#31, `keyOffset` read from `current.settings.key`), and phase order 241-348 (Key is Phase 5, behind Phases 2-4).\nProblem: the consolidated report never raised this. The riskiest feature (the worklet) cannot ship until the largest refactor (`SongQueue`, `SongFlow`, URL ownership, element reuse) has landed. The per-entry `settings` object is also speculative. By D10 (lines 119-121) key and vocal level carry over from song to song and rate resets, so nothing ever differs per song. Key and vocalLevel are session state, exactly like `vocalLevel` is today (`App.tsx:83`).\nFix: hold `keyOffset` in App next to `vocalLevel`; carry-over then comes for free and the queue just does not reset it. Drop `SongEntry.settings`; `advance()` only resets rate. Reorder to: Vocals, then Key (with its spike), then Queue and Intro. Key then has no dependency on Phases 2-4, and each slice can be reverted on its own.\n\n2. Blocker. Conflict: B2 (\"keep the gate out of the engine\") versus the design's `setPlayGate` (lines 108, 282). B2 wins.\nReason: the gate adds the most engine state (`playToken`, `pendingPlay`, `gated` in the snapshot) to the one component every consumer depends on. It also breaks every reader of `playingFlag` (toggle, Space, `k`, ThumbBar), and the engine would have to learn about queues, which is the wrong layer.\nRequired refinements to B2:\n(a) The pre-roll is App-level state, not an engine mode: one `introActive` boolean plus the card's timer. When the countdown ends or the user taps, the card calls the existing `engine.play()`.\n(b) Do the iOS prime inside the user's tap (muted `play()`, then pause, then seek 0, on the same element). Without it, calling `play()` from a timer is the Q2 failure on the very first song. Move the iOS check to the phase that introduces the intro, not the queue phase.\n(c) Pause, Seek and Restart simply dismiss the card.\n(d) Delete the `gated` snapshot field and the engine fixture on lines 294 and 282.\n\n3. Major. Conflict: M2 (\"apply `keyOffset` in `buildTimeline`\") versus B5's `effectiveKey`. Keep the single `effectiveKey`, but M2's mechanism is wrong.\nScope: lines 97-102, 339; `App.tsx:109`, `useKaraokeSession.ts:10`, `PitchLane.tsx:63,105-110`.\nProblems found in the code:\n(i) `timeline` is a `useMemo` and `KaraokeSession` is created `useMemo(() => new KaraokeSession(timeline), [timeline])`. The guide and demo synths are also rebuilt on `timeline` (`App.tsx:198-217`). Rebuilding the timeline on every key tap would therefore reset the running score and tear down both synths mid-song.\n(ii) `PitchLane` reads raw `lines[].syllables` and `s.note`, not `timeline.flat`. A transposed `flat` would not reach it.\nFix: transposition is applied at one read point without rebuilding. Give the timeline a settable `keyOffset` with a `noteOf(syl)` accessor (the `session.setScoring(...)` pattern already in the codebase), and make PitchLane, GuideSynth, DemoSynth and scoring call it.\n`effectiveKey` is the only value ever passed in: 0 when the source is YouTube/Listen, when the worklet failed or is bypassed, or when `setKey` has not yet resolved; otherwise the user's key. For the demo track it equals the user's key (synth only). Because the worklet loads asynchronously, the pitch lane and scoring must not move until the shifter is actually in the graph.\n\n4. Major. Conflict: M1 (one flag) versus G5 (every flag off equals today). Resolve by narrowing G5 and ungating Vocals and Queue.\nScope: lines 34, 137, 183, 189, 269.\nProblem: G5 promises a guarantee the design cannot keep. Phase 2 is explicitly ungated (line 269), and the Vocals slice moves Restart (a visible behaviour change). A flag cannot make \"exactly as today\" true for those.\nFix: G5 becomes \"with `VITE_FEATURE_KEY` off, the audio path (graph topology, `preservesPitch`, clock) is identical to today\". Vocals and Queue ship ungated and are guarded by the baseline flows, not flags. `VITE_FEATURE_KEY` defaults to 0 on web and native until Q1 and Q3 are measured; today the web default is 1 while Q1 says \"before it defaults on\". Delete the `?feature=` override. State plainly that native rollback is a store release, not a redeploy.\n\n5. Major. Conflict: B4 (one persistent `<video>` for every file) versus the existing `audio-element` testid flows. B4's fix loses; its diagnosis is half right.\nScope: lines 123, 290; `MediaElement.tsx:20-28`.\nReasoning:\n(a) The design's stated reason for one element (a memory \"leak\", line 162) is weak. `mediaSources` is a `WeakMap` and unreferenced elements are collected.\n(b) The real reason is iOS element activation (Q2): a fresh element has no prior user-initiated play, so `advance()` autoplay can be blocked.\n(c) Switching every MP3 user to a `<video>` element changes the default path, against the \"key 0 equals today\" rule, to cover a mixed-kind set.\n(d) The `audio-element` testid is defined only in `MediaElement.tsx`. The consumers live in the scratchpad flows that Step 0 copies in, so check them there.\nFix: keep the audio/video split and key the element by kind instead of URL, so same-kind songs (the common case) swap `src` on one element and keep one chain. A mixed-kind switch accepts the already-planned \"tap to play\" fallback on iOS. Rewrite Alternative D's reason to iOS activation. In either option the engine still needs an explicit reset on `src` swap, because `attachMedia` returns early on the same element (`PlaybackEngine.ts` `attachMedia`) and would not bump `generation`, `ended` or `lastMediaTime`.\n\n6. Major. B1 (latency) is right, but the fix should live in the engine frame, not the graph.\nScope: lines 31, 93, 337; the eight sites reading `f.time + offset` (`Karaoke.tsx:225`, `SpectrumCanvas.tsx:53`, `PitchLane.tsx:55`, `PitchTracker.ts:56`, `GuideSynth.ts:134`, `DemoSynth.ts:131`, `KaraokeSession.ts:222`) plus ProgressRow.\nFix: one shared `lyricTime(frame, track)` helper in `model/timeline.ts`. The engine exposes `setOutputLatency(seconds)` and writes it into `Frame` (already in song-seconds, so rate scaling is handled once). The helper then needs no graph reference. The tracker already has a setter pattern (`tracker.setOffset`, `App.tsx:123`); give it the same treatment. Drop \"unaffected by construction\" (line 18), since the design does change the compensated time. Treat the 30 ms goal as unconfirmed until the Q3 measurement, and measure at 0.5×, 1× and 2×.\n\n7. Major. The whole Key slice has no go/no-go gate and no fallback.\nScope: lines 90-91, 327-348, Q3 (line 50).\nProblem: three unproven assumptions carry G2. The first is that `preservesPitch = false` plus a rate mirror behaves correctly behind a `MediaElementSource`. The second is that the group delay is a stable constant. The third is that the worklet is not too heavy for a phone. The design only measures these at the end of Phase 5, after the queue refactor is done, and says what to do only if the worklet fails to load, not if it works poorly.\nFix: make a spike the first step of the Key slice (Chrome, Firefox, iOS Safari, Android; pitch error in cents at 0.5×, 1× and 2×; the latency click test). State the exit: if the spike fails, Key ships only on the demo track (synth, no worklet) or is dropped, and Vocals and Queue ship regardless. This is also where B5 resolves: drop the in-worklet auto-bypass (`performance.now` does not exist in the worklet scope) and keep one manual \"Key off\" path.\n\n8. Major. `SongQueue` as a state-owning class, plus `SongFlow`, is the wrong altitude for what the app needs.\nScope: lines 26, 105-123, 269-296.\nProblem: `source` and `track` are plain App states changed in several places (`App.tsx:90,70`). The design adds a class, a snapshot, a hook, a reducer and `setCurrentSong`, then converts four callers. `SongFlow` has six phases, but `finished`, `gradeReadyKey` and `gradeDismissed` already exist (`App.tsx:85,140,629`) and are only restated (B3).\nFix: a minimal `useSongSet` hook holding `{ entries: {file, track, title}[], index }`, which owns the single object URL and cap checks, and nothing else. Non-queue flows (`handleYouTube`, `startListen`, file upload) keep `setSource` and `setTrack`; add a per-call-site table. `SongFlow` collapses to the `introActive` flag from finding 2 beside the existing session flags. The Phase 2 \"net-neutral App.tsx\" cap (719 lines) cannot hold; replace it with a rule of no new state in App beyond the hook.\n\n9. Major. Scope still beyond the stated goals after the fixes.\nScope: lines 133, 135-137, 98-100 (hotkeys), 345, 261, 283.\nCut or defer: the `Diagnostics` module with its export UI, `window.__lg` and JSON download (use console counters and a dev-only dump); the `PitchShifter` interface (contradicts the Non-Goal on line 43; use a concrete `SoundTouchShifter`); the `v`, `-` and `=` hotkeys; `?feature=`; move-up and move-down buttons in the queue (remove and re-add covers 8 entries); code-level bodies in the plan (the `read()` helper, `toggleVocals`, the `play()` body) that belong in the PR, not the design.\nKeep: the latency click-test script (it measures Q3) and the three small UI changes.\nAlso fix one scope rule: the intro should appear only when a set is started, not for every single-file Play. Line 298 (\"gates the card for single songs too\") makes every Play wait about 2 s, which changes the default experience and has no flag after finding 4.\n\nOTHER CONFLICT CHECKS (no separate finding needed)\n- B5 `effectiveKey` and M2 agree once finding 3's mechanism is used.\n- B6 (flows do not exist) overlaps finding 7: write the flows from the assertions already listed (lines 262-263, 306-307, 322-323, 343-344) before relying on \"baseline unchanged\" gates.\n\nDOES THE DESIGN SOLVE THE ROOT PROBLEM IN THE SIMPLEST WAY AFTER FIXES?\nYes for Vocals (a bar and level toggle on an existing value), and yes for Key if the worklet passes the spike, since there is no simpler way to keep tempo while changing pitch (Alternative A moves tempo; verified in `PlaybackEngine` rate handling). Queue and Intro become simple once reduced to: one hook, one boolean for the card, the existing `engine.play()` at countdown end, and an element keyed by kind. The remaining cost is the new runtime dependency, which contradicts the README line \"no runtime dependencies beyond React\" (README.md line 5). That needs an explicit decision paragraph, a README edit, an `import()` for lazy loading, and the precache decision for the processor file, rather than a silent change.\n\nRelevant files:\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/egm/karafun-set/design_draft.md\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/src/App.tsx (lines 83, 85, 90, 109-123, 140, 198-217, 629)\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/src/engine/PlaybackEngine.ts\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/src/engine/KaraokeSession.ts (line 222)\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/src/engine/useKaraokeSession.ts (line 10)\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/src/engine/AudioGraph.ts\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/src/components/MediaElement.tsx\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/src/components/PitchLane.tsx (lines 63, 105-110)\n- /home/user/DeepSeek-R1/lyricglow/src/model/timeline.ts"}},"serverClassifierRequest":"ee245d4f-5eb8-43a8-9421-e82bfa14ee9c","apiBlockIndex":1,"requestId":"req_011Cgv383ptRKa8KAGKqBKdt","requestedModel":"claude-sonnet-5-5","promptCacheRequested":"5m","attributionAgent":"Explore","type":"assistant","uuid":"5398796e-584d-4198-85b7-568fd8a51733","timestamp":"2026-11-10T22:30:13.195Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote_mobile","cwd":"/home/user/DeepSeek-R1","sessionId":"48275c69-5936-5c57-b5a4-9fb9a672450d","version":"2.1.322","gitBranch":"claude/lyricglow-karaoke-app-ugIUj","slug":"was-just-thinking-if-synchronous-plum"}
